- **Selection method — deliberately not the previous pass's.** The 07:46Z pass ranked by slug/description similarity, exhausted it, and recommended looking for *shared arguments rather than shared titles*. This pass implemented that: extracted author-year citation keys from every eligible article's body and ranked by **citation-set Jaccard**, which finds articles making the same argument under unlike names. It works as a detector — it surfaced pairs whose slugs share no token at all (`anaesthesia-and-the-consciousness-interface` / `active-reboot`, J=0.52; `fitness-beats-truth` / `naturally-occluded`, J=0.38) that the slug method could never have reached.
- **The structural finding — the recommended signal is exhausted too, and for the same reason.** Citation-set overlap in this corpus marks *deliberate layering*, not redundancy — the identical result the slug method returned, reached by an independent route. Two orthogonal confirmations:
  1. **Every** same-section pair above J=0.19 is **mutually cross-linked in body prose**, and in each case the shorter article states its own division of labour in-text. Verbatim: `intrinsic-nature` — "This article deliberately stops at the distinction and the placement question; the theory and its difficulties… are treated in [[russellian-monism]]"; `sorkin-delta-brain-internal-analogues` — the companion "leaves the design unspecified… This article is the design space for one"; `voluntary-attention-control-mechanisms` — "This article examines *how*—the specific neural machinery"; `ethics-of-consciousness-invertebrate-question` — "This article asks the follow-up question: what do we owe these beings?"
  2. **Inverse test.** Filtering to same-section overlapping pairs that do **not** cross-link left 26, none above J=0.273, and every one shares only the corpus's generic decoherence-objection backbone (Tegmark 2000, Hagan 2002, McKemmish 2009, Reimers 2009) — apparatus cited across dozens of articles, not a duplicated argument. **There is no unaware-duplication seam.** Shared citations track a shared literature; the Map's articles divide that literature by job.
- **Length arithmetic confirms the 2026-07-28 21:28 finding at the argument-overlap grain** (merged size at 15–25% dedup vs section hard ceiling; concepts 3500, topics 4000): `binding-problem`+`combination-problem` **132–149%**; `brain-internal-born-rule-testing`+`sorkin-delta` **118–133%**; `dream-consciousness`+`incubation-effect` **113–128%**; `fitness-beats-truth`+`naturally-occluded` and `quiddity-epiphenomenalism`+`russellian-monism` **108–122%**; `intrinsic-nature`+`russellian-monism` and `attention-as-interface`+`voluntary-attention-control` **106–120%**. - **Verified-healthy structure — do not "fix" these in a later pass.** All nine pairs below are *deliberately differentiated* and were checked at the level of body prose, not metadata. Recorded so subsequent similarity sweeps (by slug, description, or citation set) can stop at this entry rather than re-triaging: the three-level Russellian chain (`intrinsic-nature` → `russellian-monism` → `quiddity-epiphenomenalism-and-the-contingency-thesis`); the Born-rule pair (`brain-internal-born-rule-testing` criterion → `sorkin-delta-brain-internal-analogues` design space); the invertebrate pair (interface-test → ethics); the attention layering (`attention-as-interface` hypothesis / `structure-of-attention` taxonomy / `voluntary-attention-control-mechanisms` neural implementation); `fitness-beats-truth` (formal theorem) / `naturally-occluded` (void category it anchors); `binding-problem` / `combination-problem`.

## 2026-07-29 13:29 UTC - deep-review
- **Status**: Success
- **File**: [[topics/amplification-mechanisms-consciousness-physics]]
- **Word count**: 3929 → 3987 (+58; 13 under the 4000 topics hard ceiling)
- **Critical issues addressed**: 3
- **Medium issues addressed**: 4
- **Enhancements made**: 1 (Stage 2 damping note)
- **Lens**: empirical-claim fidelity (does the paraphrase match what the study **found**?) — chosen because the citation-**metadata** axis was exhausted by the 2026-06-02 full publisher-of-record ledger and the 2026-07-06 re-verify. Eighth deep review; the seventh was a clean no-op.
- **Primary defect**: [[reviews/deep-review-2026-07-29-amplification-mechanisms-consciousness-physics|citation-framing inversion]]. The article read *"wakefulness is near-critical, anaesthesia pushes the brain subcritical, and epilepsy represents supercriticality (Priesemann et al., 2014)"* — but that paper is titled *"Spike avalanches in vivo suggest a driven, **slightly subcritical** brain state"* and its abstract states *"neural activity does not reflect a SOC state but a slightly sub-critical regime"*. The cited paper's central claim is that the **awake** cortex is slightly subcritical: the direct negation of the sentence citing it. It also reports no wake/anaesthesia criticality contrast (*"all in vivo avalanche distributions were similar"*). Verified at Frontiers, DOI 10.3389/fnsys.2014.00108. Survived **seven** prior reviews, all of which confirmed the metadata as correct — because it *was* correct.
- **Fix**: split into two correctly-attributed claims. The states-of-consciousness correlation re-attributed to **Toker et al. (2022)**, *PNAS* 119(7):e2024455119 (waking dynamics near the edge-of-chaos critical point, transitioning away under anaesthesia and during seizures), full author list verified at PMID 35145021. Priesemann now cited for its actual finding (driven, slightly subcritical regime; safety margin from supercritical runaway). Toker deliberately **not** restated in sub/supercritical language — its edge-of-chaos axis does not map onto the avalanche branching-ratio axis, and doing so would swap one misframing for another.
- **Downstream propagation repaired**: three passages inherited the false "cortex sits exactly at criticality" premise — *"maintains itself near criticality—not above, not below, but at the edge"*, *"systems at criticality are **maximally** sensitive"*, and *"power-law exponent **matching theoretical predictions for systems at criticality**"* (the exact claim Priesemann contests). All corrected.
- **Calibration gain**: Stage 2 of the three-stage chain rests on critical susceptibility, so a slightly subcritical operating point damps cascades. Stage 2 now says so: *"cascades are damped, so the available gain is finite rather than unbounded."* Moves the article **down** the confidence scale where evidence warrants — the inverse of possibility/probability slippage.
- **Medium fixes**: Mainen & Sejnowski 1995 relabelled ("a related result outside the SR paradigm") — it is neither a sensory-modality nor an SR study and was framed as SR evidence; Benzi et al. 1981 "first demonstrated in climate models" → "first proposed to explain ice-age periodicity" (it is the theoretical introduction); Chakroun et al. 2023 dropped qualifier restored ("at least in the male participants tested" — the study title says *in males*); "anesthetic gases **selectively** bind tubulin" → "bind tubulin".
- **Corpus check**: `grep -rn "Priesemann" obsidian/ --include=*.md` (reviews excluded) — the misframing appears **only** in this article. No propagation to repair.
- **Superlative sweep**: `find_superlative_claims` → 0 matches. No currency exposure.
- **Length discipline**: +58 net absorbed by deliberate trims (redundant "brain's chaotic dynamics serve as a natural amplifier" sentence; "acting forward in time" pleonasm beside "non-retrocausal"; compression of the SOC-coupling and criticality-tuning paragraphs). Toker abbreviated to three-plus-*et al.* in the reference list purely for the ceiling; full list recorded in the review archive. **Article is now effectively at its ceiling — route any future substantive fix to a human length decision.**
- **Attribution**: `ai_system` **held** at `claude-opus-4-6` (targeted correction, not re-authoring — matching the 2026-06-02 precedent, which also fixed citations without flipping attribution); `ai_contribution` held at 100. `ai_modified` and `last_deep_review` stamped from live `date -u` (13:29:29 UTC) — no forward drift.

(1995), "Parsimony and the Mind"*, an undergraduate senior-seminar paper, for the claim that *"'simplicity' itself has no clear and agreed-upon definition, so an argument whose key term is undefined cannot have demonstrative force."* Web-verification at the publisher turned up a second defect: **Hubbard does not assert that claim — he raises it and answers it.** The text at newdualism.org/papers/J.Hubbard/simple.shtml (and the tk421.net mirror) reads *"There are several ways of criticizing of the use of parsimony as a guiding principle… It could be argued that there is no clear and agreed-upon definition of 'parsimony' or 'simplicity.'"* — after which he stipulates three senses (epistemological, ontological, linguistic) precisely to defuse it. The Map was citing a considered-and-rejected objection as the source's own conclusion. This is the citation-framing-accuracy failure mode: real source, near-verbatim phrase, wrong dialectical role.

It now reads as a secondary pointer with its actual dialectical shape shown: *"Hubbard (1995) raises exactly that criticism for the mind-body case; he answers it by stipulating three senses of parsimony, but still concludes the principle cannot settle the debate either way."* The concessive framing is stronger evidence than the misattribution was — a defender who must stipulate definitions to keep the argument alive concedes the bare term is underdetermined. ## 2026-07-29 09:11 UTC - refine-draft
- **Status**: Success
- **Files**: [[concepts/working-memory]] (primary), [[concepts/consciousness-as-amplifier]], [[concepts/baseline-cognition]], [[apex/minds-without-words]] — assigned four-file sweep completed in one pass; nothing deferred. **Plus two unlisted loci found by a corpus-wide grep and fixed in the same pass** (below), for six files total.
- **The contradiction, and why neither side won.** `working-memory.md` L73 attributed "approximately 2±1 items" to Inoue & Matsuzawa (2007); `consciousness-as-amplifier.md` L67 cited the *same paper* for chimps "briefly holding sequences of 9 numerals". The prior pass flagged this as a possible inversion. Web-verify shows **both framings were wrong about the source**, and the resolution is neither figure: Inoue & Matsuzawa (2007) reports **no working-memory capacity estimate at all**.
- **Web-verify (publisher route blocked, so verified through the peer-reviewed review of record).** `cell.com`, ScienceDirect, PubMed and Europe PMC all returned 403/CAPTCHA for the 2007 note. Crossref confirmed metadata (*Current Biology* 17(23), R1004–R1005, DOI 10.1016/j.cub.2007.10.027). The paper's *content* was established from two independent non-circular sources: (a) **Matsuzawa's own account** of the study (Inference Review, "Primate Memory") — "Suppose that there are five numerals—2, 3, 5, 8, and 9—displayed on the touch screen. After 650 milliseconds (ms), 430 ms, or 210 ms, all the numerals are automatically replaced by white squares"; (b) the **full text of Read, Manrique & Walker (2022)**, retrieved as the accepted manuscript from the Universidad de Zaragoza repository and read locally — pp. 11 describe the task and reanalyse the data. `unfinishablemap.org` was excluded from every search to avoid self-confirmation.
- **What the 2007 paper actually shows.** A limited-hold masking task (650/430/210 ms). One extensively-trained juvenile, **Ayumu**, points correctly to the ordinal order of numeral locations at ~80% accuracy at the shortest exposure; two other chimpanzees score 67% and 62% at n=4. Young chimps beat their mothers and beat untrained humans. No capacity-in-items figure appears anywhere.
- **Where 2±1 actually comes from — the real fix.** **Read, D.W., Manrique, H.M. & Walker, M.J. (2022)**, *Neuroscience & Biobehavioral Reviews* 134, 104496 (DOI 10.1016/j.neubiorev.2021.12.019; Crossref-confirmed, author order Read-first). Abstract: *"we review empirical evidence and conclude that the size of WM in chimpanzees is 2 ± 1 versus Miller's famous 7 ± 2 in humans"*; the paper also gives the Cowan-framed pairing (chimp 2±1 vs human 4±1), so the corpus's two human comparators are both sourced. The figure is a **review's synthesis**, never an experimental result — so the corpus had been citing an experiment for a number that experiment does not contain, for at least six months.
- **Why the "9 numerals" figure is not a rival estimate.** Read et al. argue Ayumu's performance is not working memory at all: they attribute it (following Matsuzawa 2013:444) to *"eidetic imagery"* or *"some form of camera-like memory"*, and conclude *"there is neither any evidence showing that Ayumu's performance is a measure of the size of HWM nor that Ayumu has working memory capacities not matched or exceeded by humans."* So `consciousness-as-amplifier.md`'s "estimates vary" register was **rejected rather than inherited** — the literature is not mixed between two capacity numbers; there is one capacity estimate plus a famous result measuring a different thing. Composing to the model wording would have preserved a false symmetry.
- **Practice confound verified and sharpened.** Silberberg & Kearns (2009) was already in the corpus but under-specified. Read et al. give the number: humans following Ayumu's own training protocol *"perform correctly 90 % of the time, which is statistically greater than the 80 % correct performance of Ayumu"* (Cook & Wilson 2010; see also Silberberg & Kearns 2009). Cook & Wilson added as a new reference in `working-memory.md` and `minds-without-words.md`.

Papineau's actual position now stated (closure historically and scientifically motivated, not a priori) before the criticism, so the criticism attaches to what the premise *does* in the debate rather than to a position he does not hold. Papineau's **probabilistic** formulation installed: physical state plus laws fix the probability *distribution* even where no unique outcome is determined, so a non-physical cause that systematically selects outcomes violates closure in its probabilistic form — hence "indeterminacy buys logical room, not evidence", which retires the article's previous gesture at quantum indeterminacy as if it were an answer. **Horn taken explicitly**: of redundancy / overdetermination / probability-alteration the Map takes the third, in the restricted form already at `concepts/causal-closure.md` (selection within the Born distribution, confined to neurally prepared events), with the distinguishing test named — Born statistics in brain-relevant quantum events conditioned on cognitive state — and the honest note that nobody has run it.

Checked against the corpus's established Kleiner reading first (`concepts/tenet-falsification-conditions.md` cites Kleiner & Hoel 2021; `concepts/consciousness-physics-interface-formalism.md` and `apex/dualism-cartography.md` cite Kleiner 2020 on experience space) — this is a **third, distinct** Kleiner paper, so no conflict with existing usage. **The honest residual Gemini missed is stated in the article**: the indistinguishability result cuts at the Map, since minimal-interaction dualism aims by design at close to the practical indistinguishability K&H treat as the defect — which is why `tenet-falsification-conditions` has to state conditions explicitly rather than let them follow from the interaction's existence.

L61: heterophenomenology no longer "arguably reduces first-person data to behavioural evidence"; Dennett's method is stated as treating subjects as indispensable collaborators and compiling a neutral catalogue of how things seem, **bracketing** whether the seemings are as reported rather than **denying** they are data — with the Map's objection relocated to what the bracketing costs.   - **Straight numeral substitution ("seven" → "eight to nine") at 19 loci** as dispatched: [[topics/death-and-consciousness]] L163 · [[topics/amplification-mechanisms-consciousness-physics]] L59 · [[topics/quantum-holism-and-phenomenal-unity]] L128 · [[topics/time-symmetric-selection-mechanism]] L129 · [[topics/consciousness-in-smeared-quantum-states]] L96 · [[topics/interaction-problem-across-traditions]] L97 · [[topics/quantum-neural-timing-constraints]] L63 · [[concepts/predictive-processing]] L187 · [[concepts/consciousness-selecting-neural-patterns]] L104 · [[concepts/quantum-probability-consciousness]] L144 · [[concepts/mind-matter-interface]] L144 · [[concepts/prebiotic-collapse]] L146 · [[concepts/moral-responsibility]] L137 · [[concepts/motor-selection]] L178 · [[concepts/substance-property-dualism]] L127 · [[concepts/conservation-laws-and-mental-causation]] L124 · [[concepts/entanglement-binding-hypothesis]] L76 · [[concepts/timing-gap-problem]] L60 · [[arguments/materialism-argument]] L99.   - **[[topics/neural-implementation-specifics]] L62 — recomputed against a stated baseline.** Different comparison: radical-pair coherence of 10⁻⁶ s against Tegmark's femtosecond figure. Now "**nine** orders of magnitude longer than the femtosecond (10⁻¹⁵ s) scale Tegmark's calculations assign to neural superpositions—and **fourteen** orders longer than his 10⁻²⁰-second figure for ion channels specifically."   - **[[concepts/post-decoherence-selection]] L81 — "seven" → "nine", singular not a range.** The sentence's 10³ residual ("a factor of roughly a thousand") is *correct* for 10⁻⁴ s against ~10⁻¹ s decision times, and that residual entails a **nine**-order narrowing.   - **[[topics/testing-consciousness-collapse]] L159 — mechanism and figure both corrected.** The article attributed the ~10⁻⁵ s figure to "Debye-layer screening and ordered water", but those are Hagan's *additional* +3-order mechanisms (reaching 10⁻²–10⁻¹ s), not the source of 10⁻⁵–10⁻⁴ s.   - **[[concepts/mental-causation-and-downward-causation]] L127 — 23rd locus, not on the dispatch list.** Found by grepping the variant phrasing "seven orders **longer**" (no "of magnitude"), which the dispatch grep missed. - **Two dependent-arithmetic fixes beyond pure substitution**, made because the substitution would otherwise have *created* an in-sentence contradiction where none existed before:
  - [[topics/time-symmetric-selection-mechanism]] L129 also said "the corrected figure is still on the order of 10⁻⁶ seconds … roughly five to six orders of magnitude short of the 10²–10³ millisecond neural timescales". 10⁻⁶ s was consistent with "seven" but not with "eight to nine". Now "still only 10⁻⁵ to 10⁻⁴ seconds … roughly **three to five** orders of magnitude short" (10⁻⁴ s vs 0.1 s = 3; 10⁻⁵ s vs 1 s = 5). The paragraph's argumentative point — that the coherence figure alone does not close the gap and the Zeno mechanism carries the load — is unchanged and still holds on the corrected numbers.
  - [[concepts/mind-matter-interface]] L144 also said "even the favourable 10⁻⁷-second estimate"; that figure is not Hagan's and became plainly wrong once the correction was stated as eight-to-nine orders. Now "the favourable 10⁻⁵-to-10⁻⁴-second estimate". The clause's claim (that it would suffice only for faster microtubule dynamics) survives the change.

Its "seven orders of magnitude **narrower**" is the conscious-bandwidth claim (10–50 bit/s against ~10⁹ bit/s), a different claim with a different fix. This is the causal account of the anomaly, and it is why the absent differentiation prose is *not* evidence of redundancy.

## 2026-07-28 15:05 UTC - INCIDENT: a completed fork reverted its own already-committed work
- **Status**: Detected and fully recovered; no content lost.
- **What happened.** The deep-review fork `ab96c4bf53b59c011` reported at 14:56, and its work was verified and committed as `323dfca2`. It then **kept running** — its two web-verification sub-agents were still finishing — and at ~15:05 executed `git checkout 323dfca21^ -- obsidian/topics/consciousness-and-moral-agency-under-duress.md obsidian/workflow/changelog.md` followed by `rm -f obsidian/reviews/deep-review-2026-07-28-consciousness-and-moral-agency-under-duress.md`. That is a deliberate checkout of **the parent of its own commit**, plus deletion of its own review archive. The reversion was left **staged**, so the next `cycle_post` would have committed it and silently undone the review.
- **Why it did it.** Almost certainly benign intent: the skill contract says to leave changes uncommitted for the orchestrator, and on re-examining the working tree the fork appears to have "cleaned up" what it took to be its own uncommitted edits — not knowing they had already been committed. It then **re-applied** its work at 15:07:40, producing content byte-identical to the committed version apart from two refreshed timestamps.
- **Recovery.** Driver restored the article, changelog and review archive from HEAD, then re-verified: 2654 words, Morgan re-scoping present, both Feder citations present, the "among the strongest evidence" comparative still absent, `ai_system` intact. The fork's later re-application was allowed to stand and its timestamps reverted to the accurate 14:56 values, leaving zero net diff on that file.
- **Collateral damage, repaired.** The changelog restore also wiped the concurrently-running positions-evolve fork's own entry. It has been reconstructed above from that fork's report.
- **The general hazard, worth carrying forward.** A fork whose deliverable has been committed may still be alive — sub-agents outlive the parent's report — and can revert committed work while believing it is tidying uncommitted state. **Do not treat a fork's final report as proof it has stopped touching the repo.** Check the working tree immediately before `cycle_post`, and be suspicious of a `D` on a file that was committed minutes earlier. The `git checkout <commit>^ --` shape is the tell.

Full essay text retrieved and searched: Stockdale is **strikingly articulate** about the mechanism — Epictetus and the dichotomy of control quoted directly, codified resistance rules all were obliged to memorise, daily ritual of prayer/exercise/tap code — and reports that after release most prisoners were found to have met the pressure in much the same way. The seeding passage was **reversed**: his "traits of susceptibility which I don't think psychologists yet have words for" is about why men **collapsed**, not how any **resisted**. Rewrote so Stockdale functions as the constraint he is: resisters *can* explain themselves, the genuine opacity sits on the susceptibility side, and the Map's claim narrows to the step from sustained commitment to *this* act of endurance. Also noted honestly that Stockdale concluded against Descartes that body and mind are inseparable.

Gemini mis-routed the finding to `inverted-qualia.md`; the quoted text is at `concepts/visual-consciousness.md` L45 and L53, and this was the one finding in the Gemini leg that survived verification as genuine and correctly-routed once re-targeted. Driver re-verified the zero-hit PP grep on disk before dispatch.
- **Word count**: 1816 → 2024 (+208); `ok` both sides, concepts soft threshold 2500, ~476 words of headroom remaining. No condensing needed or done.
- **Route taken: (b), recalibration + honest boundary-marking. Route (a) was considered and rejected on the merits.** Route (a) would have required naming a feature of spatial phenomenology the interface model accounts for that sensorimotor-contingency and precision-weighted-prediction accounts do not. No such feature is available: PP/active inference models the construction of egocentric depth representation, and sensorimotor-contingency theory models the immediacy, so the only residue the Map can name is the generic hard-problem question of whether the built representation is felt — which is not a discriminator but exactly the framework-boundary disagreement. Claiming it as one would have replaced an overclaim with a different overclaim. Route (b) is also what [[project/evidential-status-discipline]]'s Strongest-Physicalist-Rival Gate prescribes: where the physicalist framework natively models the phenomenon, the honest register is boundary-marking, not in-framework refutation.
- **The defect fixed (L45)**: the paragraph closed "the phenomenal depth, solidity, and extension of the visual world remain unexplained by the computational account" — a strong negative existential claim about the computational literature, made without naming the dominant computational framework claiming to explain exactly this. That clause is removed. A new paragraph names predictive processing and active inference explicitly, concedes what they account for (egocentric centring, depth gradients, perspectival layout from sensor fusion and precision-weighted prediction error; sensorimotor contingency explaining the immediacy, since subpersonal inference is not experienced as inference), states that spatial behaviour and the discrimination profile are computationally accounted for, narrows the contested residue to whether such a representation is felt or merely reports being felt, and marks the boundary honestly by noting no current measurement discriminates the two.
- **Consistency check that decided the framing**: [[topics/three-dimensional-world-representation-problem]] — the very article L45 links to — already carries the honest calibration ("Friston-style active-inference models routinely derive perspectival representations from sensor-fusion principles; whether those representations are accompanied by felt three-dimensionality is exactly the question at issue"). L45's flat "remain unexplained" was out of step with its own linked sibling. The new prose inherits the sibling's framing rather than inventing one.
- **L53 (inverted spectrum), decided on the merits — treated, but lightly.** The conditional structure ("If this scenario is coherent") was already a hedge, but "neither would know" was asserted flatly and the antecedent was presented as granted. Now: "the standard argument runs that neither would know", plus a sentence stating the antecedent is contested rather than granted, naming representationalist and illusionist denials and the direction of twenty-first-century colour science, and routing to [[concepts/inverted-qualia]] for the full replies. Deliberately kept to two sentences — the dedicated article owns this argument.
- **Verified against the sibling article before writing, not assumed.** The review's Verification Notes attributed to `inverted-qualia.md` L160 the phrase "would likely reveal themselves under careful testing." `grep` on disk returns **zero hits** — that phrase is not in the article. Had it been copied across it would have been a fabricated internal quote. What the article actually holds (L78, L82–84, L173) is that the inversion argument is *modal, not empirical*, that the twenty-first-century record (Hofer et al. 2005; Webster et al. 2000; Emery et al. 2023; Horiuchi & Nagai 2024) has not gone the argument's way, and that variation surfaces in the judgements making observers functionally non-identical. Tenet 4 is not retracted or weakened; the cell records only that this cluster is not among its supports.

They shared a founding move (Hepburn's art-versus-X disanalogy; the same "disinterested contemplation, the framed aesthetic object, the free play of imagination" triad verbatim), a shared figure (Berleant), and near-duplicate `Relation to Site Perspective` sections — the Occam's Razor paragraphs were the same sentence pattern twice, and both closed on the same "the literature both feeds and complicates the Map's case" line. - **Why this pass found what two prior passes did not**: the 2026-07-19 review explicitly *skipped* verification under the §2.4 "References block unmodified" rule. That was true when written — then commit `7d434ab86 auto(coalesce)` merged [[concepts/epistemicism-about-consciousness]] in and grew the reference list from 8 entries to 15. The inherited 07-11 ledger covered only the original 8; the seven coalesce-introduced entries (Williamson 1994, Sorensen 1988, Sorensen 2001, Jago 2012, SEP Sorites, two Map self-cites) had never been verified in any pass. Both critical defects live in that block. **A coalesce invalidates an inherited citation ledger** — the skip rule is unsafe across one.
- **Critical 1 — wrong-work attribution**: "absolute borderline cases" was credited to Sorensen's *Blindspots* (1988). It is a chapter of *Vagueness and Contradiction* (OUP, 2001), confirmed at the Oxford Academic chapter page and corroborated by NDPR (absolute/relative at Ch. 1.2, truthmaker treatment at Ch. 1.11). Right author, right view, wrong work — re-attributed, not deleted. The article's *gloss* of the term was independently correct and was kept.
- **Critical 2 — reversed author position**: the same sentence had Sorensen "treating the sorites as a paradox about the *word* rather than the world". Backwards. Per SEP §3.2 and Weatherson's review, use-grounding is *Williamson's* signature move and Sorensen explicitly rejects it, objecting that it makes definiteness relative to a discriminator — which is exactly why he needs *absolute* borderline cases. This contradicted the article twice over: four sentences earlier it has Sorensen holding that "vagueness pervades the way the world is divided", and its own Fact-Maker Problem section correctly identifies his route as truthmaker-*gap* (no truth-maker at all — not use-facts). Clause deleted; the genuine shared core kept; the divergence now named with a forward pointer to where it does work.
- **Critical 3 — over-generalized premise**: "a truth-maker that *generic epistemicism* got for free from use" → "that *Williamson's version* got for free from use". Generic epistemicism did not get it for free from use; Sorensen denies truth-makers outright, as the next paragraph says. The narrower claim is the true one and is what the argument needs.
- **Medium 4 — empirical-claim fidelity**: "Jago (2012) argues this is unstable" sharpened to name the actual objection — truthmaker-gap epistemicism cannot accommodate the higher-order vagueness Sorensen himself insists on. Strengthens the Map's reason for declining the route: the objection is internal to Sorensen, not imported.
- **Quote fidelity**: the one quoted string — Williamson's "small differences in meaning, not to small differences in the objects under discussion" (pp. 230–231) — verified **verbatim** against Sainsbury's review, which quotes the full sentence and cites pp. 230–1. Exact match; page range correct.
- **Metadata fix**: Jago 2012 reference was missing volume/pages; corrected to *Thought: A Journal of Philosophy*, 1, 320–329 (DOI confirmed at Wiley). SEP §3.2 section number *and* exact title "The Epistemic Theory" confirmed live.
- **No fabricated citations.** 0 superlative-currency candidates. Map self-cite pseudonyms (Oquatre-huit, Oquatre-sept) retained per corpus convention. No `[1m]` artifact.
- **Subject-specific checks**: no epistemic/metaphysical slippage — the article holds the sharp-metaphysically / hidden-epistemically distinction throughout and never lets "no detectable difference" drift into "no difference"; the "no external measure" passage grounds an epistemic conclusion in a metaphysical premise but says so explicitly.   - **Palmer framing corrected (strand 1).** "Palmer (1999) argues certain inversions—like flipping red-green while leaving blue-yellow fixed—are plausible within color science" was an over-read of a real source. Palmer's published abstract concludes that literal spectrum reversal would *not* work and that three other transformations *might*, conditional on which colour categories matter. Rewritten as a conditional opening rather than a result, with the author-stance note that Palmer's "isomorphism constraint" is a sceptical limit on what colour science can settle about experience, not a licence for the inversion argument, and that his candidate transformations were contested within the same *BBS* exchange. Same defect class and same register as tonight's Shoemaker and Hardin repairs; no deletion.
  - **New "Twenty-First-Century Colour Science" subsection (strand 2, the 3/3 convergent finding).** Four results, framed as Claude's leg framed them — large physiological variation coexisting with appearance that does not track it is *prima facie* evidence against strong inversion. Foveal L:M cone ratios 1.1:1 to 16.5:1 among colour-normal observers (Hofer et al. 2005); unique hues "largely unconstrained by normal individual differences in the cone-opponent axes" (Webster et al. 2000); interobserver hue-scaling variation resolving into several *local* factors rather than one global factor, the wrong shape for a whole-space opponent-axis rotation (Emery et al. 2023); discrimination training shifting thresholds and judged appearance together, so appearance is functionally coupled (Horiuchi & Nagai 2024). States explicitly that measurable individual difference is not evidence for strict inversion, because the differences surface precisely in the judgements that make observers functionally non-identical, which supports the structuralist reading. The article's reply is identified as the modal claim rather than a rival reading of the data — no suggestion that the empirical case has been answered.
  - **Hilbert & Kalderon added (strand 3, one sentence).** Framed to their verified abstract — extant versions of the inverted spectrum argument are unsound and any possible version must be — rather than to the reviewer's paraphrase about all possible quality spaces being asymmetrical, which the abstract does not state. Closes the retreat to stipulated symmetric aliens.
- **Deferred**: strand 4 (representationalism narrow/wide content, appearance properties, Fregean modes, colour relativism; PCS depth via Zhong 2024) — the most expensive and least convergent strand, and the length budget would not carry it honestly. Owed as its own task.

Hilbert & Kalderon 2000 in Davis (ed.) *Color Perception*, VSCS 9 — REAL, abstract claim re-framed to what it actually says. ## 2026-07-28T02:16:16+00:00 - refine-draft
- **Status**: Success
- **File**: [[concepts/luck-objection]]
- **Word count**: 2167 → 2301 (+134); hard threshold 3500, so ~1200 words of headroom retained. Growth confined to the one calibrated tenet entry; no other section touched.
- **Scope**: MWI/authorship calibration only, target (7) of a consolidated multi-file task, L148. The other five targets were deliberately not swept.
- **Locus verified before editing**: L148 read verbatim as quoted in the brief — "If all branches are equally real (MWI), no selection occurs—every outcome actualizes somewhere."
- **The defect**: "no selection occurs" is a flat authorship claim that branch-relative Everettian accounts (Saunders 2010; Wallace 2012; Sebens & Carroll 2018) defeat on their own terms — they preserve branch-local psychological continuity, counterfactual control and reasons-responsiveness, so an Everettian can say a selection did occur in this branch, flowing from this agent's reasons. The article asserted as derived what the Map in fact posits.
- **Why this article makes the fix unusually worth getting right**: the luck objection *is* the charge that an undetermined choice is random rather than authored. Under branching the Everettian's reply is genuinely strong — reasons-responsiveness survives branch-relatively, so the choice is not lucky in the sense the objection normally targets. The honest statement, now installed, is that branching converts the luck objection from a question about *randomness* into a question about *exclusion*: what makes this outcome the agent's own rather than a counterpart's becomes a bare indexical fact. That is a sharper and more useful claim than "no selection occurs" and it strengthens the article.
- **The fix**: inherited the settled exclusion formulation from `apex/consciousness-and-agency.md` (tenet block) and `concepts/quantum-indeterminacy-free-will.md` L141 — Everettian grant of a branch-local history of having chosen; "counterfactual exclusion authorship requires"; "every rejected option is actualised by a counterpart elsewhere". Posit-marker and boundary-marker in the settled form: globally-nonactual unchosen is "a [[tenets/background-commitments|posit the Map adopts]] rather than a conclusion the response to luck delivers, and the disagreement sits at the framework boundary." No new formulation invented; no new references added, since the Everettian literature is cited once at `tenets/background-commitments.md` and the siblings link there rather than re-citing.
- **Calibration ground**: authorship/selection family, so the exclusion posit — *not* the indexical/measure-weighting move reserved for suffering and moral-weight claims.
- **Engagement classification** (editor-internal, not in article prose): engagement with the Everettian — Mode Three, framework-boundary marking. The prior text was a Mode One overreach (asserted that selection itself fails under branching) that the Everettian's own resources defeat; the reply now concedes the branch-local restatement and marks the exclusion demand as a Map posit rather than an in-framework refutation.
- **Calibration, not retraction**: Tenet 4 stands unweakened and the positive conclusion survives verbatim — collapse is real, consciousness participates in it, genuine choice results. `ai_system` held at `claude-opus-4-5-20251101` (calibration edit, not re-authoring), matching how the siblings were handled. `tenets/background-commitments.md` untouched, per brief.

## 2026-07-28T01:09:55+00:00 - deep-review
- **Status**: Success
- **File**: [[topics/interface-efficacy-and-the-cognitive-gap]]
- **Word count**: 3437 → 3637 (+200); soft 3000, hard 4000 — soft_warning, comfortably below hard. Growth is entirely citation apparatus (4 new references) plus the prose attaching them; no argument expansion.
- **Critical issues addressed**: 3
- **Medium issues addressed**: 1
- **Enhancements made**: 4 verified references added
- **Targeting premise corrected**: the brief asserted no prior sidecar contained a citation ledger. False — the 2026-06-25 review has a full "Publisher-of-Record Citation Ledger (§2.4)" section. The real gap, visible inside that ledger's own wording, was that **8 of 24 references were carried on trust** ("not independently re-fetched this pass", "verified in prior reviews") and the 3 Map self-cites appeared in no ledger at any date. All 11 fetched this pass; citation surface now genuinely closed.
- **Critical 1 — New York Declaration corporate-authorship error**: ref 10 listed "New York University, York University, & London School of Economics" as authors. The Declaration has three named individual authors (Andrews, Birch, Sebo) and explicitly states signatories sign "in their personal capacity and not on behalf of any institution." Corrected, canonical URL added; the two quoted phrases re-verified verbatim at source.
- **Critical 2 — Roth & Dicke paraphrase, wrong comparison class**: article had their paradox as "humans vs monkeys"; their actual paradox compares elephants/cetaceans against **great apes**. The substituted comparison does not follow from their own intelligence ordering. Restated.
- **Critical 3 — Von Economo neuron claim uncited and taxonomically stale**: the assertion carried no citation at all, in the article's most citation-dense paragraph, and ended in an unsourced comparative ("more cleanly than raw cortical-neuron count"). Attached Nimchinsky et al. 1999 (within-hominid gradient) and Cauda et al. 2014 (VENs since found in macaques, backdating primate origin ~15→~25 Mya); dropped the unsupported comparative. Removes a brain-side prop the article treated as firmer than it is.
- **Medium — two-decade-stale neuron counts**: Roth & Dicke's 2005 figures re-measured in opposite directions since. Herculano-Houzel et al. 2014: African elephant cortex 5.6B, ~1/3 human (elephant anomaly retires). Mortensen et al. 2014: long-finned pilot whale neocortex 37.2B, ~2× human (cetacean anomaly deepens). Both cited; downstream "in the human range" → "at or above the human range". Net effect on the argument is honest and mildly favourable — loses the elephant case, gains a strictly stronger cetacean case.
- **Fabrications found**: none. Every defect was wrong-metadata, wrong-paraphrase, or missing-citation.
- **False-positive averted**: DeWall et al. 2008 nearly reported as a claim-reversal on a PubMed-abstract reading suggesting no unconscious-load condition existed. Retrieving the author's full PDF showed "nonconscious load" is the paper's **own term for its own Experiment 1 condition**, and the article's paraphrase tracks the authors' framing exactly. Instance of [[citation-verify-false-negative]]; settled at primary text, not at the abstract.
- **Circularity guard**: unfinishablemap.org surfaced in search results during that check and was not used.
- **Currency helper coverage note**: `find_superlative_claims` returned EMPTY, yet this pass found two real currency defects. Both were stale *numbers* and a superseded *taxonomic scoping*, not superlative phrasing — the helper detects phrasing, not staleness.
- **Slippage diagnostic**: passes, fourth consecutive review. No calibration hedges touched; edits are citation-layer only.
- **Engagement classification**: unchanged and not load-bearing — Gutfreund 2024: Mode Three, honest (symmetric ceiling applied to the Map's own hypothesis); brain-side reading positioned alongside, not against; NYD structural compatibility without endorsement. No editor-vocabulary leakage in prose.
- **Residual risk relocated**: bibliography now fully verified, but all three defects this pass lived *downstream* of metadata. Highest-yield future lens on this article is the **uncited empirical assertion** — the sentence that sounds sourced because its neighbours are — not the reference list.
- **Output**: [[reviews/deep-review-2026-07-28-interface-efficacy-and-the-cognitive-gap]]

## 2026-07-27T22:56:00+00:00 - deep-review
- **Status**: Success
- **File**: [[topics/phenomenology-of-memory-and-the-self]]
- **Word count**: 3219 → 3338 (+119, 83% of the 4000 hard threshold)
- **Critical issues addressed**: 4
- **Medium issues addressed**: 3 (plus 3 low)
- **Selection**: merit-based pick, not from the candidate ranking (top-8 contaminated by same-day cosmetic bumps). 29 references, 31 days since last review.
- **Ran the §2.4 web-verify pass against its own trigger.** The 2026-06-26 review had skipped it on the "body/References unmodified" rule and inherited the prior ledger. Running it anyway found four critical and three medium defects on a surface six reviews had declared converged — direct evidence the trigger rule is **inverted for converged articles**: stability suppresses verification, so the most-reviewed articles carry the least-checked citation surfaces, while inherited ledgers report them as verified.
- **Critical (1)** Klein (2012, 2014) misattributed as documenting AD/MCI patients sustained by *semantic* continuity. Klein reasons from severe episodic amnesia (D.B., K.C., Zasetsky), not dementia, and concludes that a **pre-reflective felt continuity of first-person subjectivity** does the work — not semantic content. Wrong population *and* wrong mechanism. Provenance: the grouping entered via an outer-review task note (Claude Opus 4.7, 2026-05-10) and was transcribed without independent check. Split: Tippett et al. now carries the AD/MCI semantic finding (verified verbatim against PMC5826309); Klein re-scoped to what he argues. The article's conclusion **strengthens** — Klein's route is phenomenological, not informational.
- **Critical (2)** Matthen (2010) and Russell (2014) mislabelled "affective readings" locating pastness in "finality, nostalgia, regret". Neither holds this: Matthen locates the felt character in spatiotemporal embeddedness and ergonomic significance, Russell in felt remoteness. Relabelled "felt-quality readings" and described accurately; the "absorbable" verdict survives and is better supported.

## 2026-07-27T16:06:55+00:00 - deep-review
- **Status**: Success
- **File**: [[topics/chinese-philosophy-of-mind]]
- **Task**: Verbatim-quote + translation-fidelity + doctrinal-attribution sweep against primary-text and publisher scholarship. The sole prior sidecar (2026-07-07) web-verified the five *secondary* citations only and never ran a primary-text pass on the classical Chinese terms and doctrines. Genuine unchecked surface — **not a no-op**.
- **Primary lens, reversal check — NO REVERSAL.** The Zhu Xi / Wang Yangming pairing was checked specifically for the failure mode found earlier this session in the Madhyamaka/Yogācāra material (a pairing that reads plausibly but runs backwards). Verified at the SEP: Wang holds principle is already complete in the heart-mind, Zhu Xi holds it is found by investigating things outside — the article had it the right way round. *liangzhi* correctly attributed to Wang. **All eight Chinese character strings individually matched to their romanisations** (氣/qi, 理/li, 道/Dao, 心/xin, 格物/gewu, 良知/liangzhi, 心即理/xin ji li, 知行合一/zhi xing he yi): no mismatches.
- **CRITICAL — unrestricted negative, fixed by calibration.** The headline claim, carried in lead, description and basin-edge section, was that Chinese thought *never* divided mind from matter. Falsified inside the tradition by the *xing*–*shen* (形/神) controversy of the 4th–6th centuries: Fan Zhen's *Shenmie lun* (神滅論, 507) argued spirit stands to body as sharpness to a blade, and drew some sixty commissioned replies under Emperor Wu of Liang. New section "The Body and Spirit Debate" states the exception and shows why the argument survives it — both sides argued in the shared vocabulary of *qi*, the dispute turned on *persistence* rather than constitution, nobody asked why a process should be accompanied by anything it is like, and Fan Zhen's own analogy is an inseparability thesis of the organicist kind. The scoped claim now defended: the central categories offered no place to *lodge* an explanatory gap. Note the article was the **outlier in its own cluster** — both siblings already hedge with "largely"; only this one carried the unhedged form.
- **CRITICAL — coverage gap.** An article titled "Chinese Philosophy of Mind" treated only the indigenous strands, with no acknowledgement that Chinese Buddhism imported an Indian analysis of mind wholesale (Faxiang transmitting Yogācāra after Xuanzang's seventh-century return). Scoping paragraph added, Buddhist material routed to [[eastern-philosophy-consciousness]].
- **Citation ledger — false negative caught mid-pass.** An intermediate page fetch reported the SEP "Zhu Xi" author as Joseph A. Adler; SEP's own author-and-citation-info page gives **Kirill Thompson**, and Adler is merely cited *inside* the entry. The prior sidecar's attribution stands — recorded in Stability Notes so no future pass "corrects" it. Year 2025 added for parallelism with Van Norden 2024 (both re-confirmed at the publisher). Needham's "organic naturalism"/"organic materialism" both verified as his terms. **Wong, Soo Lam (2025), *Dao* 24(1):105–117, DOI 10.1007/s11712-024-09978-z added — Crossref-verified** (Springer landing page is auth-walled).
- **Quote fidelity — no fabricated verbatim quotations, nothing needed de-quoting.** Quoted strings are term-glosses functioning as standard renderings, not attributed verbatim quotation. Two translation-fidelity fixes: *liangzhi* re-attributed from "usually rendered 'innate knowing' or 'pure knowing'" (contested renderings presented as consensus, and a misstatement of Chan) to "'innate knowledge' by Chan and 'pure knowing' by Van Norden"; and the jade metaphor corrected — the article had transposed *veins* from the leaf onto the jade, where Thompson's entry has "the grains in wood, the lines in jade, the 'veins' in a leaf".
- **SEP framing check.** Both entries support what they are cited for. Two omissions repaired: Zhu Xi's *ontological priority* of *li* (the closest thing in his system to a two-category structure, i.e. precisely what the article claims the vocabulary lacks) is now stated and disposed of rather than dropped; and *gewu* is no longer presented as Zhu Xi's coinage — it is a phrase from the *Great Learning*, and Wang's re-reading of it as *rectifying* things sharpens the article's own contrast. No circular verification: plato.stanford.edu, Crossref and publisher pages only, never unfinishablemap.org.
- **Engagement**: no named-opponent replies in the body; §2.6 N/A. No label leakage in the new prose.
- **Word count**: 2108 → 2640 (+532), topics/ soft 3000, status `ok`. Growth buys a named counterexample the article previously papered over; no offsetting cuts required below threshold.
- **Critical issues addressed**: 2
- **Medium issues addressed**: 4
- **Enhancements made**: 4

## 2026-07-27T15:37:17+00:00 - deep-review
- **Status**: Success
- **File**: [[topics/reactive-attitudes-and-strawsonian-responsibility]]
- **Task**: Verbatim-quote + attribution sweep. The sole prior sidecar (2026-07-09) was an argument-lens cross-review with a citation *metadata* ledger; quoted strings had never been checked. Genuine unchecked surface — **not a no-op**.
- **Primary source**: the British Academy's own scan of the bound volume, `thebritishacademy.ac.uk/documents/4837/48p187.pdf`. 25-page image scan with no text layer, so it was read page by page as images. No aggregator, no quote site, no Map self-citation used to verify or "correct" any string.
- **Both long quotations verify VERBATIM and are in the 1962 title essay, not a neighbouring piece of the 1974 Methuen collection.** The suspected different-essay trap did not materialise. "the very great importance that we attach to the attitudes and intentions towards us of other human beings…" — p. 190, Strawson's "central commonplace". "part of the general framework of human life, not something that can come up for review as particular cases can come up for review within this general framework" — pp. 198–199.
- **Referent drift on quote 2, corrected**: Strawson's subject is "This commitment" — our natural human commitment to ordinary inter-personal attitudes — not the reactive framework itself. Re-anchored.
- **Four of six short quoted pleas were paraphrase in quotation marks.** Verified against pp. 192–193. "he didn't realise" → 'He hadn't realized'; "he's a young child" → 'He's only a child'; "he's psychologically incapacitated" (not Strawson's at all) → 'He's a hopeless schizophrenic'; "he was under extreme and unusual stress" → 'He has been under very great strain recently'. "he didn't mean to" and "he was pushed" were genuine; restored to Strawson's case and italics. Substance was correct throughout, so nothing was deleted — the strings were restored to his own wording, which is no longer than the paraphrases.
- **Wallace phrase DE-QUOTED**: the practices "make perfectly good sense even if determinism is true" traces to Harvard University Press catalogue copy, not verifiably to Wallace's own prose. Substance and citation retained as paraphrase.
- **Page range corrected — critical**: cited as *Proceedings of the British Academy* 48, **1–25**; the bound volume runs **187–211** (first page 187, running heads 188…210, final page 211, signature "C 187" at the foot of p. 193). The 1–25 form is the separately issued lecture offprint and circulates widely (SEP uses it), so the reference now gives volume pagination, notes the offprint form, and warns the Methuen/Routledge reprint paginates differently again. Page cites added to both long quotations.
- **Argument fidelity — Strawson's second limb was missing, now added.** The article rendered the dissolution as resting on inescapability alone. Strawson's second reply (p. 199) is that even if a genuine choice were imaginable we could choose rationally "only in the light of an assessment of the gains and losses to human life, its enrichment or impoverishment", and "the truth or falsity of a general thesis of determinism would not bear on the rationality of this choice". Omitting it weakened an opponent the article undertakes to present at full strength, and made the Pereboom paragraph over-reach; that paragraph is now scoped to the premise on which the *first* limb rests.
- **Exemption scope tightened**: Strawson's two sub-groups differ — episodic versus standing, "either at the time of his action or all the time" (p. 193, verbatim). **Pereboom scoped**: his target is the *desert-based* reactive attitudes, not the reactive attitudes wholesale; reference entry added (2014, OUP) since he was named inline with none.
- **Attribution hygiene**: P.F./Galen disambiguation re-verified at all three loci — accurate, intact, no drift between father and son, preserved verbatim. Watson 1987 (Schoeman ed., CUP, ch. 11), McKenna & Russell 2008 (Ashgate, Farnham — the 2026-07-09 place fix holds), Wallace 1994 (HUP), Fischer & Ravizza 1998, Galen Strawson 1994, both SEP entries: all real-correct.
- **Engagement with Strawson**: Mode Three, and now more honestly so — the Map declines a stronger opponent than the one previously on the page. Pressure on Wallace's aptness conditions remains Mode Two adjacent, framed as "the Map's reading". No label leakage. The "declines the dissolution without claiming to refute it" framing and the "not evidence for dualism" disclaimer preserved untouched.
- **Word count**: 2581 → 2792 (+211), topics/ soft 3000. Quote restorations roughly length-neutral; growth is the second-limb paragraph plus one reference.
- **Critical issues addressed**: 6

- **Enhancements made**: 2

- **Status**: Success
- **File**: [[concepts/anomalous-monism-and-the-denial-of-strict-psychophysical-laws]]
- **Task**: P2 — verbatim-quote + attribution sweep. Genuine unchecked surface confirmed: the 07-10 sidecar was a cross-review that verified all 8 bibliographic tuples but never checked a single quoted string. Quote fidelity is orthogonal to citation metadata, and that is exactly what this pass found.
- **Word count**: 1929 → 2061 (+132; soft 2500, status `ok`)
- **Critical issues addressed**: 2 — (1) **wrong-work attribution**: `"have no echo in physical theory"` was cited to "Mental Events" (1970); the phrase occurs nowhere in that essay (zero grep hits across the full reproduced text) and is in fact from Davidson's **"Psychology as Philosophy" (1974)**, Essay 12 of the same 1980 collection — confirmed by text extraction of the Oxford Scholarship Online chapter PDF (DOI 10.1093/0199246270.003.0012). Re-attributed per the verbatim-quote-cited-to-wrong-work disposition (re-attribute, never delete), the quotation extended to include Davidson's load-bearing "rough correlations" clause, and a new References entry added (S. C. Brown ed., *Philosophy of Psychology*, 41-52, Macmillan 1974; repr. *EAE* ch. 12, 229-244). A knock-on interpolation was fixed with it: the article's gloss "coherence, consistency, **charity**" replaced by Davidson's own verbatim triple "conditions of coherence, rationality, and consistency" — *charity* is Davidsonian but not in this sentence. (2) **verbatim punctuation error** in the cause-law principle: Davidson has a **colon** ("there must be a law: events related as cause and effect…"), the article had a semicolon.
- **Task flag disconfirmed**: the queue entry flagged `"Principle of the Nomological Character of Causally Relevant Properties"` as a probable Davidson-misnaming. **False positive.** The article never attributes it to Davidson — it correctly presents it as Honderich's proposed reframing, and SEP's *Anomalous Monism* supplement confirms verbatim that "Honderich suggests that the cause-law principle must be articulated slightly differently—as the Principle of the Nomological Character *of Causally Relevant Properties*." Davidson's own principle is separately and correctly named in the numbered list. No change; recorded as settled so it is not re-opened.
- **Medium issues addressed**: 3 — (1) **Honderich's illustration**: the article's invented rock/window example sat immediately after "His point:", where it read as Honderich's own. Replaced with his actual example, confirmed at both IEP and SEP: green pears on a grocer's scale, truly describable as green and as French, but not caused by those properties to read one pound. (2) **Sosa's gunshot example de-attributed**: six searches across SEP (*Mental Causation*, *Anomalous Monism* + supplements), IEP, and PhilPapers failed to attach the loud-gunshot example to Sosa 1984 at any publisher-grade source. Not a fabricated citation — Sosa 1984 is real, correctly cited, and SEP §5.2 confirms his role in the causal-irrelevance literature — so the cite and argument are kept and only the specific example is no longer credited to him. Conservative disposition, reversible if the paywalled primary text ever confirms it. (3) **McLaughlin 1989 re-framed**: "argues that anomalous monism collapses into type epiphenomenalism" over-committed him and risked recruiting a defender as an objector — he also argues that being efficacious in virtue of physical properties does not entail being efficacious *only* in virtue of them, and IEP cites his 1993 "On Davidson's Response to the Charge of Epiphenomenalism". Now credits him with the taxonomy that gives the charge its sharp form, with his non-hostility noted.
- **Verified real-correct, deliberately unchanged**: the Anomalism-of-the-Mental quotation (verbatim, word for word); Davidson's "Thinking Causes" characterisation and the "changes the subject" rejoinder, both confirmed at SEP *Mental Causation* §5.5 including the correct same-volume critic trio; and the supervenience-credit hedge — "often credited with introducing the term **to philosophy of mind**" is precisely scoped and would have been wrong if broadened, so it was preserved on purpose.
- **Engagement classification** (editor-internal, not in the article body): engagement with Davidson — **Mixed, dominantly Mode One**. The lever is the epiphenomenalism objection pressed from inside physicalism by Honderich, Sosa, Kim, and McLaughlin, so the disagreement is earned on Davidson's own terrain rather than by tenet-incompatibility. **Mode Three** residue declared honestly in the closing lines of "The Double-Edged Relation to the Map" (physicalist rival ontology, not a friend in different clothes). No boundary-substitution; grep for the forbidden-label set returns clean.
- **Calibration**: no possibility/probability slippage. The selection law stays "non-strict, probabilistic" throughout, Map moves are marked as proposed/speculated, and Tenet 5 is deployed *against* a too-quick preference for the Map's own view. A tenet-accepting reviewer would not flag any claim here as overstated.
- **Second lens (argument fidelity)**: the reconciliation paraphrase checks out against Davidson's §I text — principle 2 concerns events not descriptions, the covering law need only be framed in some vocabulary, token identity is *derived* not assumed, and the inconsistency is non-formal. No strawmanning; the Map disagrees with Davidson's actual extensional-causation position.
- **Generalisable finding**: a Davidson quotation sourced to "Mental Events" may actually come from a neighbouring essay in the same 1980 collection. Intra-corpus consistency checking could never have caught this, and the collection-level citation looks right at a glance. Future Davidson quotations in this corpus should be pinned to an *essay*, not to *Essays on Actions and Events*.
- **Output**: [[reviews/deep-review-2026-07-27-anomalous-monism-and-the-denial-of-strict-psychophysical-laws]]

Checked the reviewer's trilemma for exhaustiveness and it holds, on a linear-algebra fact worth stating plainly: a mixture `Σ_C P(O|C,X)·w(C)` that is invariant under *every* re-weighting `w` requires every conditional to equal the marginal. - **Critical 1 — citation-framing error, Salmon mis-framed as strengthening a test he bounded.** "Reichenbach's common-cause principle (1956), *sharpened by Salmon*" reversed Salmon's actual contribution. Web-verified at SEP (`plato.stanford.edu/entries/wesley-salmon/`; no unfinishablemap.org source used): after van Fraassen's criticism Salmon **identified limits** to screening-off and introduced **interactive forks**, where a genuine common cause *fails* to screen its effects off from each other. [[citation-framing-accuracy-lens]] class — cite real, metadata correct (Princeton UP 1984, confirmed), conclusion mis-framed. It mattered because the mis-framing flattered the article's own load-bearing test: screening-off is the article's discriminator between genuine triangulation and one architecture read N times, and Salmon's result is that *surviving* the test is weaker evidence of independence than the clean form suggests. Two tells that it was AI-introduced: the sibling [[comparative-phenomenology-of-meditative-traditions]] that the sentence paraphrases never mentions Yogācāra, and the pairing is doctrinally reversed. Corrected to "Madhyamaka's dissolution of the witness against Yogācāra's reflexive *svasaṃvedana*", which converts the error into a sharper instance of the informative-divergence point the sentence was making.

Author order, venue, volume, issue, pages and year all **confirmed**; the task text's metadata was correct. - **Review context**: 8th deep review; priority lens = quote fidelity, run *fresh* rather than inherited per the cycle driver's "do not no-op on ledger-complete grounds" instruction. The 07-12 ledger listed 10 quotes as verified, but two entries were inherited from an earlier ledger and one was corrected by inference (no primary render obtainable). This pass pulled full primary texts directly — Gutenberg plain-text of *Principles* Vols 1–2 (57628/57634) and *Essays in Radical Empiricism* (32547), Wikisource *Human Immortality* — and grep-verified every quoted string. Both critical defects lived inside entries the prior ledger called verified: **completeness was mistaken for soundness.**
- **Changes**:
  - **(1) CRITICAL — wrong-work attribution re-attributed.** "Thoughts and things are absolutely homogeneous as to their material…" was positioned as the 1904 "Does 'Consciousness' Exist?". Grep of the complete ERE text (which *contains* the 1904 essay as Essay I) shows the phrase occurs **exactly once in the whole book**, in **Essay V, "The Place of Affectional Facts in a World of Pure Experience"** (JPPSM vol. II no. 11, 25 May 1905) — James's own retrospective summary of the 1904 essay. Right author, right substance, wrong work. Re-attributed (not deleted) with a lead-in pinning the 1905 essay; also restored the silently dropped second "that" ("and *that* their opposition"), which had converted a reported clause into a free-standing assertion, and marked the lift with a leading ellipsis. Covered by the existing 1912 ERE reference.
  - **(2) CRITICAL — word put in James's mouth removed.** "Neutral monism tensions" argued from *James's own verbs—"reducing", "permitting", "transmitting"*. Full-text count of *Human Immortality*: `reducing` = **0**; `reduce` = 1, in an unrelated passage about population. James's actual vocabulary is `permissive` (×2), `transmissive` (×5), `permits`, `obstructiveness`, and the prism analogy's "sifted and limited". "Reducing valve" is Bergson's/Huxley's — which the article itself says three sections earlier, so it was internally inconsistent; corpus grep confirms every other file credits Bergson/Huxley (the 07-17 terminal-lucidity review made the same call). Replaced with James's real language and added a one-clause provenance guard against reintroduction. The argument is unweakened and now actually evidenced.
  - **(3) Inference upgraded to primary proof.** The 07-12 fix `innumerably`→`innumerable` was made on convergent inference because "every full-text host truncates before Chapter XI's ending." Gutenberg 57628 is not truncated: line 17805 reads "…forged innumerable ages ago", inside Ch. XI (15972–18400), in the free-will close of the Attention chapter — the correction and the article's chapter lead-in are both confirmed. Entry closed on evidence.
  - **(4) Claim-fidelity addition — second-edition preface.** The neutral-monism section leans on "mother-sea" as evidence of a pre-existing reservoir. James's Preface to the Second Edition, not previously consulted, bears directly on it: he lets the reservoir stand but disowns the monistic reading, writing he is "anything but a pantheist of the monistic pattern" and had spoken of the mother-sea as a unit only "for simplicity's sake." Added compactly (both fragments verbatim-verified); sharpens the section's own thesis and matters to the Map, which commits to a mental substance rather than a pantheistic unit.
  - **(5) Emphasis restored.** James italicised *attend* in "the essential achievement of the will… is to *attend* to a difficult object"; the article had flattened it. Restored — the emphasis is the point of the sentence.
  - **Self-contamination guard.** The first WebSearch for the "Thoughts and things" quote returned **unfinishablemap.org as top source**, paraphrasing the Map's own article back as confirmation. Discarded; all verification re-run against primary full texts with Map domains blocked. Accepting it would have carried the wrong-work attribution through a ninth review.
